## F-132. Base and Celo block builders read through the reth execution cache that still has the stale branch for a destroyed account without code, when the operator enables cache sharing

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: sequencer, validator or bridge committee. Networks: Celo.

What differs on the network: Celo's endpoint still reports reth/v2.2.0-74cc8bb, the celo-org/reth revision with the old insert_state branch for a destroyed account whose original info had no code. The block builder reads through the execution cache only when the engine is started with --engine.share-execution-cache-with-payload-builder, whose default is false.

How it meets the code: 1. Nothing about the code path changed since cycle 14: the endpoint reports the same build and celo-reth v1.0.10 only patched tracing. 2. The public node setup that Celo publishes starts celo-reth without the sharing flag, so follower and RPC nodes built from it never hand the cache to a builder. The sequencer's own start parameters are not published. 3. The PoolManager's storage is never on the faulty branch, because it has code from deployment and cannot be destroyed, and transient storage (src/libraries/CurrencyDelta.sol:21-39, src/libraries/CurrencyReserves.sol:17-36) is not cached across transactions. Only a contract created in the block that destroyed its codeless address can read a stale word, so only an attacker-made token or hook could be affected, and such a contract can misreport balances to sync and settle (src/PoolManager.sol:279-288, 349-364) without any cache fault.

What is at risk: No funds held by the PoolManager for other users move. If the flag is on at the sequencer and the trigger works, unsafe blocks could be published with a state that followers reject, and swaps and liquidity changes in them would be re-executed after the reorg.

What it takes: The Celo sequencer operator runs with cache sharing enabled, and some read in an ordinary block caches a word for a codeless address that a later transaction in the block destroys and re-creates. Neither link is confirmed.

What can be done: Celo upgrades celo-org/reth to a revision with the reth pull request 28148 fix, or keeps sharing off. Integrators wait for safe or finalized blocks before treating a fill as final.

This entry adds to F-124.

Rated low until cycle 17. The builder with cache sharing on reads through the same revm state and journal rules as the default path and adds no slot prefetch, so it takes no path the default lacks (lead L-807). Rated info, the same as F-124 and F-133, which rest on the same upstream branch.

Second reviewer's corrections: The title still names Base, while this unit's entry lists only chain 42220; the Base half is held by the existing ledger entry. The follower validation path, which an unprivileged account could reach without the operator flag, is a separate existing finding (theme-reth-cache-drift, actor operator, severity info) and is not covered here.

Code: `src/PoolManager.sol:279-288`, `src/PoolManager.sol:349-364`, `src/libraries/CurrencyDelta.sol:21-39`.

Sources:

- https://forno.celo.org web3_clientVersion, 2026-11-24 (the endpoint still reports reth/v2.2.0-74cc8bb)
- https://github.com/celo-org/celo-kona/releases (v1.0.10 of 20 November is the newest celo-reth release and only fixes SELFDESTRUCT tracing)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/celo-org/celo-l2-node-docker-compose/main/scripts/start-op-reth.sh (the published node start script runs celo-reth without --engine.share-execution-cache-with-payload-builder)

## F-136. Nodes on seven networks are built with Go releases that are out of support or carry prove pass faults (issues 79874, 80517, 82839), and no EVM result was found to depend on them

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: sequencer, validator or bridge committee. Networks: Elysium, BNB Smart Chain, Polygon, Avalanche, Celo, Arbitrum One, Robinhood Chain.

What differs on the network: The Elysium endpoint reports nitro/vlocal+unknown-unknown/linux-amd64/go1.25.12. Go 1.25 left support when go1.27.0 shipped on 2026-08-19. go1.25.13 fixed, among others, issue 79874, where the prove pass emits a 32 bit slice mask for a 64 bit slice operation on targets that sign-extend 32 bit constants (amd64 named), so a slice with a start index of 2^32 or more advances too little, and issue 80517, where prove applies a fact before the point where it holds and drops a bounds check, shown on amd64 as a call through an out-of-range function table entry. Arbitrum One and Robinhood Chain run go1.27.3, which is inside the range of the separate F-117 fault. Cycle 17 additions. (1) BNB Chain: 48 Club, a validator group and block builder, answers at rpc-bsc.48.club from two unpublished geth commits (132104cd and 9b93ce23), both built on 2026-11-16 with go1.25.5, a release line out of support since 2026-08-19 and missing the compiler fixes of go1.25.6 to go1.25.13. (2) Go issue 82839, opened 2026-11-03 and still open: the prove pass carries a fact through a sign extension of a narrow integer compared with a constant that does not fit the narrow type, and takes a branch that cannot be taken. The report names go1.26.6 to go1.26.10, go1.27.3 and master, and a variant from go1.24.13. Inside that range: bor v2.11.1 (go1.26.10), BNB Chain geth v1.8.2 (go1.26.10), avalanchego 1.15.3 (go1.26.10), Celo's op-geth backend (go1.24.13), Nitro on Arbitrum One and Robinhood Chain (go1.27.3) and Elysium (go1.25.12). Cycle 18 addition: the fix for issue 82839 (CL 858581) is still unmerged, so no Go release holds it. The faulty step is in factsTable.update of the prove pass (go1.26.10 prove.go lines 1101-1117 and 1147-1157) and reads the same in go1.24.7. Whether go1.25.12, which Elysium reports, lies inside the range of issue 82839 was not established (lead L-876), and Elysium's build names no source commit, so the tree search below does not speak for it. Cycle 20 addition: Elysium's go1.25.12 and the go1.25.9 build of the consensus replay binary that all three Arbitrum chains use contain the prove pass code that Go issue 82839 reports (prove.go lines 1000 to 1120 read the same in go1.25.9, go1.25.12 and go1.26.10, and the fix CL 858581 is unmerged). Both are outside the range of issue 81863, whose trigger arrived in go1.26. The release branch logs for go1.25.6 to go1.26.11 were read: the go1.26.10 builds of bor, BNB Chain geth and avalanchego lack only the fixes for issues 81863, 81868 and 82839.

How it meets the code: Both faults change behaviour only on unusual shapes. Issue 79874 needs a slice start of 2^32 or more that the compiler has proved in bounds. The interpreter never slices calldata, code, memory or return data with such an index: a copy or load with a larger offset is clamped to the data length or charged for memory expansion first, and a zero-length copy or hash at a large offset touches no slice. The new offsets.py vectors aim at exactly that region (offsets from 2^31 to 2^256-1 for CALLDATALOAD, CALLDATACOPY, CODECOPY, KECCAK256, MCOPY and RETURNDATACOPY) and Elysium returns the specified word or failure on every one. Issue 80517 removes a bounds check whose failure would otherwise panic; on a correct program the check never fires, so it can only turn an existing out-of-range bug of the node into a silent read. The PoolManager paths that depend on these copies are the hook result copy (src/libraries/Hooks.sol:148), the revert bubbling copy (src/libraries/CustomRevert.sol:109), and the arithmetic in src/libraries/FullMath.sol:21-54; all match on the battery, the targeted set, the script sets and 3,515 mined swaps. The settled state is computed by the consensus-v61 WebAssembly machine built with go1.25.9 (F-119), which predates both fixes as well but runs on a different target. Cycle 17 additions: the EVM paths the PoolManager depends on work on 64 bit words in holiman/uint256 and the interpreter, and no comparison of the issue 82839 shape was located there, though the source was not searched systematically (lead L-839) and no binary was decompiled. The answers were tested instead on 2026-11-25: the battery (99,985 vectors), the targeted set (85,544) and the large-offset vectors match the specification on Polygon, BNB Chain, Avalanche and Celo (Celo pinned to its op-geth backend), and on the 48 Club endpoint the battery, the targeted set, the call semantics words, the slot reads and the script sets match too. Which of the two 48 Club builds answered each batch is not known (lead L-844). bor, BNB Chain geth and avalanchego at go1.26.10 already contain the fixes for issues 79874 and 80517. Cycle 18 addition: the systematic search that lead L-839 asked for was done over the same 849 files and 73 packages as F-117. The trigger is a signed ordered comparison where one side is a sign extension of an int8, int16 or int32 value of the form x plus or minus a constant and the other side is a constant outside the narrow type. The search found no such comparison, no widened narrow signed value compared with anything, and one narrow signed widening in total: uint256 v1.3.2 ExtendSign (uint256.go:1275), the SIGNEXTEND handler. That value is shifted and or-ed into the result and never compared; the branches of the function test uint64 values. uint256 v1.2.4 (Avalanche) builds the mask without a narrow conversion. 122 conversions whose operand type could not be resolved were read by hand and none has a narrow operand. 2,340 vectors per network aimed at SIGNEXTEND, sign extension chains and ADDMOD returned the specified word on the six Go client networks. Cycle 20 addition: the shape search was run over OffchainLabs/go-ethereum f1c5f02, the fork the replay binary pins, with its six Arbitrum-specific files in core/vm and core/state. It found 16 lower-tier candidates and no site of the faulty shape. The shape of issue 81868 appears in core/vm only in BitVec.setN, which passes 1,620 jump destination cases on Polygon, BNB Chain and Avalanche. Nitro's own arbos and precompiles packages, Elysium's unpublished tree and the go1.27 fix list for the Arbitrum One and Robinhood Chain builds were not read.

What is at risk: None found. A miscompiled routine in one operator's build would make that node disagree with the others, which shows up as a missed or reorganised block. On Elysium, with one whitelisted validator, the operator would be agreeing with itself.

What it takes: An operator keeps building from an affected or unsupported Go release, and some function on the interpreter or state path has one of the faulty shapes. No such shape was found. The Elysium, 48 Club and Nitro rc.9 builds are private, so those checks are by their answers.

What can be done: The deployer can ask the operators (Kinetiq for Elysium, 48 Club, and the client teams of bor, BNB Chain geth, avalanchego, Celo op-geth and Nitro) to build with a supported Go release that holds the fixes, and to publish the version and commit of each build. LPs need do nothing.

Second reviewer's corrections: go1.25.12 is two point releases behind, since go1.25.14 also shipped on 2026-08-19; the security fixes in go1.25.13 (crypto/tls, net/http, net/url and others) are an additional operational reason to rebuild, not an EVM result. The reporter of issue 80517 says it does not reproduce on 1.26.5; the exposure of 1.25 rests on the backport note in issue 80618 that the root cause is older and the new tests fail on 1.25. Issue 79874 says the upper half of the mask register is zeroed on amd64, so on amd64 the effect is truncation of the index to 32 bits.

Code: `src/libraries/Hooks.sol:130-153`, `src/libraries/CustomRevert.sol:95-118`, `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:14-54`, `src/types/Currency.sol:44-75`.

## F-143. Go releases up to go1.27.4 still carry four longstanding amd64 and arm64 wrong-code rules and an open phiopt fault, and no EVM path in the searched Go client trees has their shapes

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: sequencer, validator or bridge committee. Networks: Ethereum, Polygon, BNB Smart Chain, Avalanche, Arbitrum One, Robinhood Chain, Elysium.

What differs on the network: Arbitrum One and Robinhood Chain answer as nitro v3.13.0-rc.9 built with go1.27.3, on linux-arm64 and linux-amd64. go1.27.3 lacks the go1.27.4 deadstore fix for Go issue 81863 and the still unmerged fix for issue 82839, and it holds the 81868 fix. Every released Go version that was read (go1.25.12, go1.26.10, go1.27.3, and the go1.27 branch head) also lacks five master fixes: issue 82825, where an amd64 unbounded shift and a ==, !=, <= or > comparison of its count with the width give a wrong boolean; issue 82777, an arm64 conditional complement that becomes XOR 1; arm64 noov comparisons of a - x*y with 0 that are wrong at the minimum integer; arm64 double REVW that loses a zero extension; and issue 83036, a loop-carried boolean phi in phiopt. Ethereum mainnet answers no differently in this respect, because its Go nodes use the same releases. The difference is that these networks run only Go nodes, so no second client would reject a faulty block.

How it meets the code: A miscompile changes an EVM result only if a Go function on the opcode, state or precompile path has the faulty shape. The cycle 20 scanner was extended with five shapes and run, with type information, over holiman/uint256 v1.3.2 and v1.2.4, Nitro's go-ethereum fork at 0e44603 and f1c5f02, Nitro's arbos, arbos/programs, precompiles and util/arbmath at consensus-v61, and the geth v1.17.9, bsc v1.8.2, bor v2.11.1, Celo op-geth c7ed55b8, libevm, coreth and graft/evm trees. Results: no comparison of the 82839 shape (A1 and A2 empty, also in Nitro's own packages); no new 81863 site (four B1 hits in Nitro are heap composite literals in memory-backed state constructors and test helpers); no signed comparison of a - x*y with 0; no nested 32-bit byte reversal; every conditional complement has a constant operand, which folds to a constant and never becomes CSINV. The only D1 sites are uint256 v1.2.4 Lsh, Rsh and SRsh (uint256.go:1040, 1099, 1163), where n > 64 is a switch case. A block control is recomputed by flagalloc, and the fault reaches only materialised booleans, so these sites compile correctly. In v1.3.2, which Nitro pins, the cases are n >= 64 and the shifted count after n -= 64 is a different value. The phiopt shape appears only as the tracing flag logged in the interpreter loop, which a deferred closure captures, so it is not an SSA value. The opcode handlers that the PoolManager's SHL, SHR, SAR, SIGNEXTEND and MULMOD reach (core/vm instructions.go opSHL, opSHR, opSAR, opSignExtend and opMulmod, uint256 Lsh, Rsh, SRsh, ExtendSign and MulMod) contain none of the shapes. The vectors agree: 185,529 battery and targeted vectors and 300 pmdiff2 scripts match the specification and the Ethereum reference on Arbitrum One (arm64 only) and on Robinhood Chain (both architectures, see the cleared entry).

What is at risk: No loss path found. If such a site existed on the EVM path of one architecture, nodes of that architecture would compute a different state for an affected block, which stalls confirmations without moving funds. If it existed in the sequencer's binary, a wrong result could be canonical, which is why the opcode paths were searched.

What it takes: A Go function on the EVM path with one of the shapes, compiled by an affected release. None was found. The private rc.9 source (b72783f), the fork's hand-written assembly and the generated machine code were not read, and the go1.27.3 compiler could not be built or run here, so the search is syntactic.

What can be done: Node operators can move to go1.27.4 or later for 81863, and to the first release that carries the 82825, 82777, noov, REVW and 83036 fixes once one exists. Nitro can rerun its differential tests on both architectures for each toolchain change.

This entry adds to F-136.

Code: `src/libraries/TickMath.sol:61-63`, `src/libraries/TickMath.sol:142-145`, `src/libraries/TickBitmap.sol:20-38`, `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:24`, `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:48`.

Sources:

- https://go.dev/doc/devel/release (go1.27.0 2026-08-19, go1.27.1 2026-09-01, go1.27.2 2026-10-08, go1.27.3 2026-10-19 (crypto/x509 only), go1.27.4 2026-11-10 (compiler, linker, runtime))

## F-146. Polygon's publicnode endpoint answers from at least five unpublished bor commits, and several of them answer within the same seconds

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: sequencer, validator or bridge committee. Networks: Polygon.

What differs on the network: Ethereum endpoints serve tagged client releases. polygon-bor-rpc.publicnode.com, the RPC that networks.json lists for Polygon, reported bor/cb3d19c5f690/v2.11.1-private at 09:15 UTC and bor/280514fa9fb7/v2.11.1-private at 09:23 and 09:26 UTC on 2026-11-26. Neither commit, nor the three seen in earlier cycles, exists on raw.githubusercontent.com.

How it meets the code: An eth_call answer from this endpoint can come from any of these private builds. The battery and the targeted set ran unpinned against it and matched every vector, so the builds in rotation during the run compute the opcodes the pool math uses as specified. No source can be read for them, so a change to core/vm in any of them is known only through its answers.

What is at risk: No loss. Results from this endpoint cannot be tied to one build unless each batch is bracketed with web3_clientVersion.

What it takes: Only the endpoint operator decides which build serves a request.

What can be done: Pin opcode and script runs to a build string, or use an endpoint that reports a tagged release such as QuikNode's v2.11.1 builds.

Second reviewer's corrections: The endpoint rotates among at least four private builds, not two: in this check ebcfea01452d and a863b8e5cce1 answered within the same few seconds as cb3d19c5f690 and 280514fa9fb7. The mitigation of bracketing batches with web3_clientVersion therefore needs to handle four or more tags.

Code: `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:24`, `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:48`.

Sources:

- https://polygon-bor-rpc.publicnode.com (web3_clientVersion answers cb3d19c5f690 and 280514fa9fb7 on 2026-11-26 (research/network-risk/cycles/024/established-l1.work/clientversions.txt, clientversions-after.txt, gap137-partial.json))
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/0xPolygon/bor/cb3d19c5f690/params/version.go (HTTP 404: the commit is not in the public repository)

## F-147. Minority Ethereum execution clients run CPU-selected jump destination kernels and an erigon storage read cache that no public endpoint can be pinned to

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: sequencer, validator or bridge committee. Networks: Ethereum.

What differs on the network: Erigon 3.8 (about 3 percent of reporting validators) and Nethermind 2.2 (about 43 percent) compute the jump destination bitmap with SIMD kernels chosen from the CPU at start, so validators running one release on different hardware run different code for it. Erigon 3.8 also answers SLOAD from a two-entry per-frame cache whose invalidation rests on a read stamp of its state object. Geth and reth, which earlier cycles tested, do neither of the erigon changes. No public endpoint serves eth_call from Nethermind, and the only one that reaches erigon rotates its backend on every request.

How it meets the code: Every branch of the PoolManager is a JUMP or JUMPI to one of its 610 JUMPDEST bytes. A kernel that dropped one of them would make that branch fail on the affected client. A kernel that marked a 0x5b byte inside push data would change nothing, because the PoolManager never jumps to a computed target taken from input. The storage cache matters at PoolManager.sol:196 and 202-206 and at 154-159: the frame reads slot0 for checkPoolInitialized, calls the hook, and reads slot0 and the liquidity again inside Pool.swap or Pool.modifyLiquidity. If the stamp did not change when a hook reentered the PoolManager and moved the price, or when a nested write was reverted, the outer frame would swap from the old price. The erigon SSE4 kernel and its scalar fallback were built here from the v3.8.1 source and matched an independent scalar rule on the PoolManager bytecode (build and mainnet code, 610 destinations), on all 192 prefix and suffix cuts of each, on 400,000 random strings and on 289,510 single-push strings; a one-byte change to the kernel's JUMPDEST constant gave 1.2 million mismatches, so the harness sees a fault. The NEON kernel and the four Nethermind kernels were not run. The cache probe gave the specified words on every answer.

What is at risk: None found. A fault in a client below one third of the stake makes its nodes reject canonical blocks; it does not change what the canonical chain records for pool funds. Nethermind is above one third, so a Nethermind fault on a PoolManager path would stall finality until fixed, and funds would wait without loss. Changing the recorded state needs a fault in clients holding two thirds of the stake.

What it takes: A fault in one of the unrun kernels or in the read stamp logic, reached by a PoolManager path, on clients that together hold enough stake to matter. Nobody has to hold a role to send the transaction that reaches it, but the effect on funds needs the faulty client to dominate block production and attestation.

What can be done: Run the Nethermind JumpDestinationAnalyzer kernels and the erigon NEON kernel against the PoolManager bytecode and the scalar rule. Read the erigon ReadStamp source. Operators with a choice can keep a geth or reth node beside an erigon or Nethermind validator.

Second reviewer's corrections: clientdiversity.org shows two execution-client datasets. One gives Nethermind 43, Geth 43, Besu 8, Reth 3 and Erigon 3 percent. The other gives Geth 50.13, Nethermind 25.46, Besu 9.45, Reth 7.67 and Erigon 6.53 percent. Under the second, Nethermind is below one third, and a Nethermind-only fault would knock its nodes off the chain without stalling finality. The conclusion that no single minority client can change canonical pool state holds under both. The ReadStamp invalidation rule is still unread, and the NEON and Nethermind kernels were not run, so those parts of 'none found' rest on reading the code and on the probe.

Code: `src/PoolManager.sol:187-206`, `src/PoolManager.sol:145-159`, `src/libraries/Hooks.sol:134`.

Sources:

- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/erigontech/erigon/v3.8.1/execution/vm/analysis_amd64.s (erigon v3.8.1 jump destination kernel in SSSE3 and SSE4.1 assembly)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/erigontech/erigon/v3.8.1/execution/vm/interpreter.go (per-frame two-entry SLOAD cache keyed by ReadStamp)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/erigontech/erigon/v3.8.1/execution/vm/contract.go (jump validity read from the bitmap alone, cached by code hash)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NethermindEth/nethermind/2.2.2/src/Nethermind/Nethermind.Evm/CodeAnalysis/JumpDestinationAnalyzer.cs (Nethermind selects AVX-512 VBMI, AVX2, SSSE3 or AdvSimd kernels at run time)
- https://clientdiversity.org/ (execution client shares of reporting validators: Nethermind about 43 percent, geth 43, Besu 8, reth 3, erigon 3)
- file:research/network-risk/cycles/024/ethereum-reference.work/jdtest/jdtest.out (harness result: 689,896 cases, 0 mismatches)

## F-150. Monad's main branch rewrites the integer library and the x86 emitter in ways that would reach compiled PoolManager blocks before any eth_call can see them

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: sequencer, validator or bridge committee. Networks: Monad.

What differs on the network: Monad validators run native code that the x86 emitter produces for frequently used contracts, while every public endpoint answers eth_call and serves traces through the interpreter. The main branch, which no tag carries, changes that emitter and the 256 bit library in four places: a Barrett reduction for division, ADDMOD and MULMOD in uint256.hpp, elision of the memory bound check for literal offsets already touched in the block, direct calls to runtime routines by immediate address, and a migration to the asmjit 2 API.

How it meets the code: The PoolManager's pool math runs MULMOD with the literal modulus 2^256-1 on every mulDiv (src/libraries/FullMath.sol:24) and with a variable modulus for the low remainder (src/libraries/FullMath.sol:48, :112, src/libraries/SqrtPriceMath.sol:252). A Barrett path for a constant modulus, if a later release wired it into the MULMOD handler, would sit under all of these. Today no VM file on main calls it, and math.hpp still calls the division-based mulmod. The literal-offset memory elision on main applies to the revert encoders and the free memory pointer (src/libraries/CustomRevert.sol:16-43, src/libraries/Hooks.sol:144, src/types/Currency.sol:57), which write at offsets 0x00, 0x04 and 0x40. The emitter resets the tracked end at every block that is a jump destination, and memory never shrinks within a frame, so on reading the elision gives the same result. A wrong elision would read or write memory past the frame's allocation. The interpreter would not show it, because eth_call never runs native code (rpc/monad_executor.cpp lines 1306-1309 at v0.16.7), and tracing nodes run the interpreter for blocks too (cmd/monad/main.cpp line 488). A fault would show only as the tracing RPC nodes falling out of consensus at the delayed merkle root, three blocks later. By then the validators' result is canonical.

What is at risk: None today. No release contains these changes and no revision after MONAD_TEN is scheduled. If a release carried a fault in one of these paths and two thirds of validators ran it, every pool's swap and liquidity amounts would follow the faulty native result.

What it takes: Monad must tag and roll out a release built from main, and a fault must exist on a path the PoolManager reaches. An ordinary swap would then trigger it. No ordinary account can make validators run a given release.

What can be done: When a tag after v0.16.7 appears, rebuild the cycle 23 harness (cycles/023/theme-monad-compiled-coverage.work/tools/build.sh) at that tag. Run the six script sets and the mined transactions natively, and run a direct test of uint256.hpp's mulmod, addmod and udivrem, with the Barrett variants, against Python integers. Do this before validators adopt the tag.

Code: `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:24`, `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:48`, `src/libraries/SqrtPriceMath.sol:252`, `src/libraries/CustomRevert.sol:16-43`, `src/libraries/Hooks.sol:144`.

Sources:

- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/category-labs/monad/main/category/core/runtime/uint256.hpp (Barrett reciprocal types and barrett::mulmod, addmod, mulmod_const and udivrem overloads on main; division-based mulmod unchanged at lines 955-962)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/category-labs/monad/main/category/vm/compiler/ir/x86/emitter.cpp (touched_memory_end_ elision at lines 6340-6356 and reset at lines 1030-1045; call asmjit::imm(runtime_fun_))
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/category-labs/monad/v0.16.7/cmd/monad/main.cpp (--trace_calls selects VM::InterpreterOnly, otherwise VM::Dual (line 488))
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/category-labs/monad/v0.16.7/category/rpc/monad_executor.cpp (eth_call always uses the interpreter (lines 1306-1309))
- https://docs.monad.xyz/monad-arch/consensus/asynchronous-execution (delayed merkle root D = 3; a node with an execution error falls out of consensus)
- https://docs.monad.xyz/developer-essentials/changelog/releases (v0.16.7 is testnet only; v0.16.5 is the last mainnet release)

## F-152. Avalanche serves receipts, logs and state at latest from the answering node own execution, which consensus confirms only at settlement

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: sequencer, validator or bridge committee. Networks: Avalanche.

What differs on the network: Under SAE a block is final at acceptance, but its execution results are agreed by consensus only when a later block settles it, about five seconds after execution. latest is the last block the answering node executed; safe and finalized are the last settled block.

How it meets the code: A wallet, bridge or indexer that reads a PoolManager take, a Transfer log or a balance at latest reads the result of that node execution (sae/rpc/receipts.go:28-41, saexec/receipts.go:48-73). An honest node computes the same result as every other, because the order is fixed at acceptance and execution is deterministic. A node with a local fault (disk corruption, as the source names, or a miscompiled build) can serve a receipt or a balance that settlement later contradicts, and nothing in the RPC response marks the result as unsettled. The PoolManager itself is not affected: it settles inside one transaction (src/PoolManager.sol:104-114).

What is at risk: No loss of pool funds. An integration that releases value on a latest receipt from a faulty node could act on a result that never settles. Ordinary users of pools are not exposed.

What it takes: A faulty or dishonest RPC node, and an integration that acts on latest instead of safe or finalized.

What can be done: Bridges and off-chain settlement on Avalanche should read safe or finalized, which the SAE backend maps to the last settled block, or check the result on a second endpoint.

Second reviewer's corrections: The source names only disk corruption as the cause of a divergent execution result; a miscompiled build is the finding's own addition, reasonable but not in the cited comment. actor: operator is acceptable as the closest value for a faulty or dishonest RPC node; no ordinary account can cause it.

Code: `src/PoolManager.sol:104-114`.

Sources:

- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ava-labs/avalanchego/v1.15.3/vms/saevm/blocks/access.go (latest is the last executed block and safe and finalized are the last settled block (lines 124-147); the comment at 110-123 says settlement demonstrates consensus of execution results)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/avalanche-foundation/ACPs/main/ACPs/194-continuous-execution/README.md (blocks are accepted, then executed, then settled by a later block that carries the state root and receipts root)
- raw/probe_tags.json (eth_getBlockByNumber on https://api.avax.network/ext/bc/C/rpc) (safe and finalized lag latest by 6 to 7 blocks on the live node)

## F-154. From T14 Tempo validators vote to notarize a block before its execution is verified and withhold only the finalize vote

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: sequencer, validator or bridge committee. Networks: Tempo.

What differs on the network: Tempo v1.19.0 and v1.19.1 default --consensus.verification-mode to hardfork, which switches to deferred verification at the first epoch boundary at or after T14 (8 December 2026 15:00 UTC, timestamp 1796742000). Under deferred verification a validator checks only that the block is available, that its embedded consensus context matches and that it names the expected parent before it votes to notarize. The execution check (newPayload VALID) runs while the vote collects and gates the finalize vote.

How it meets the code: crates/consensus/src/args.rs:35-56 and 153-165 define the modes; crates/consensus/src/consensus/application/mod.rs:120-143 selects the Deferred wrapper of commonware-consensus 2026.11.0, whose module documentation (marshal/standard/deferred.rs:15-37) says the notarize vote is cast without waiting for the application to verify the block contents, and certify waits for that result before a finalize vote. If certify returns false the validator broadcasts nullify for the view and refuses to build on the block (simplex/mod.rs:106-111, 138-140), so a block whose execution is wrong cannot gather a finalization from an honest quorum and is never finalized. Nothing in the source read slashes the proposer. The executor of an RPC node moves HEAD to a notarized convergence target only after its own newPayload returned VALID and its ancestry reaches the network finalized tip; otherwise HEAD is the finalized block, and safe and finalized are always the finalized block (crates/consensus/src/executor/actor.rs:12-25 and 2884-2889). A wallet reading latest on such a node therefore reads a block the node itself executed correctly, which a nullified view can still replace. The PoolManager settles inside one transaction (src/PoolManager.sol:104-114), so no PoolManager state outlives the replaced block.

What is at risk: No loss of pool funds. After T14 an integration acting on latest can, in a view that is notarized and then nullified, see a PoolManager take that does not reach the final chain. A wrong arithmetic result in one validator's build cannot be finalized unless a quorum shares it, as before T14.

What it takes: Tempo T14 active, a validator set that notarizes and then nullifies a view, and an integration that acts on latest. Only validators act.

What can be done: Integrations on Tempo should read finalized (equal to safe) after T14. A day-one sample of latest against finalized should confirm whether the public endpoint moves HEAD past finalized.

Second reviewer's corrections: Deferred verification is not the only way a block served at latest on Tempo can be replaced after T14. A notarized block can be followed by a nullification in simplex under either mode, so the integration hazard comes from the executor's HEAD policy. Deferred mode itself cannot put an invalid block at latest on an RPC node, because that node checks VALID first. That supports severity info.

Code: `src/PoolManager.sol:104-114`.

Sources:

- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tempoxyz/tempo/a9d6748/crates/consensus/src/args.rs (VerificationMode Immediate completes application verification before voting to notarize, Deferred defers it until voting to finalize, Hardfork follows T14 (lines 35-56, 153-165))
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tempoxyz/tempo/a9d6748/crates/consensus/src/consensus/application/mod.rs (the factory hands each epoch the Deferred or Inline marshal wrapper by the previous epoch boundary timestamp (lines 1-12, 120-143))
- https://static.crates.io/crates/commonware-consensus/commonware-consensus-2026.11.0.crate (src/marshal/standard/deferred.rs, src/simplex/mod.rs) (notarize vote without waiting for application verification; certify gates the finalize vote; certify false leads to nullify and no child built on the block)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tempoxyz/tempo/a9d6748/crates/consensus/src/executor/actor.rs (HEAD becomes a notarized convergence target only after the node's own newPayload VALID; safe and finalized hashes are the finalized block (lines 12-25, 2884-2889))
- https://tempo.com/developers/docs/changelog (v1.19.0 is required for T14 at 1796742000 and the verification mode defaults to hardfork)
- https://tempo.com/developers/docs/protocol/tips/tip-1031 (the consensus context in the header exists for deferred verification, and a validator may keep synchronous verification)
- theme-deferred-verification.work/raw/tempo_heads.json (eth_getBlockByNumber on https://rpc.mainnet.tempo.xyz, tempo/v1.19.1-a9d6748 before and after) (400 blocks 50,949,238 to 50,949,638 read at latest kept their hash; latest never ran ahead of finalized by more than the time between two queries)

## F-156. The public OP Mainnet endpoint answers the elements of one batch from two op-reth builds, so a batch bracket does not pin an eth_call to a build

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: sequencer, validator or bridge committee. Networks: Optimism.

What differs on the network: Ethereum reference endpoints used in this review answer from one build per request. mainnet.optimism.io routes each element of a JSON-RPC batch on its own, between op-reth/v2.8.1-72207294 and an unversioned op-reth/v0.0.0-dev-unknown.

How it meets the code: In 30 batches of three web3_clientVersion calls sent on 2026-11-26, 18 batches mixed the two builds, 11 named only the unversioned build and 1 named only v2.8.1. A batch that puts web3_clientVersion before and after an eth_call therefore does not show which build ran the call. Every opcode vector and script on OP Mainnet in this and earlier cycles ran on one of the two builds, and every one returned the specified word, so neither build was seen to compute FullMath, TickMath, BitMath or the settlement path differently. What is not shown is that each build alone ran every vector. The four rounds of the new 15,510 vector set reduce the chance that a batch never reached one build, but do not exclude it. The PoolManager computes what the block producer computes; the endpoint builds only answer reads.

What is at risk: No funds are at risk. The evidence for OP Mainnet is unpinned per build, and the unversioned build has no published source to read.

What it takes: None for the routing itself. A fault would also need one of the two builds to compute an opcode differently and to be the one that produces or validates blocks.

What can be done: Run pinned checks against a single-backend endpoint or a self-hosted op-reth of each version, and ask the operator which commit the unversioned build carries.

Code: `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:24-48`, `src/libraries/TickMath.sol:61-111`.

Sources:

- https://mainnet.optimism.io (batched web3_clientVersion answers recorded in cycles/027/op-stack-eth.work/batch-clientver.txt)

## F-159. Polygon has one execution client that can follow mainnet, because the 0xPolygon/erigon line stopped before the Austin fork

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: sequencer, validator or bridge committee. Networks: Polygon.

What differs on the network: Ethereum has several execution clients, so an arithmetic fault in one is outvoted. On Polygon the last 0xPolygon/erigon tag is v3.7.2, whose mainnet chain spec ends at Valencia (block 89,531,000). Austin, Uburu and Salvador are missing, so every Polygon validator and full node runs bor.

How it meets the code: Every swap computes FullMath.mulDiv, which calls MULMOD with modulus 2^256-1 (src/libraries/FullMath.sol:24) and, when the product needs more than 256 bits, with the pool's denominator (src/libraries/FullMath.sol:48). On Polygon, opMulmod is the only implementation of that result. The current bor v2.11.1 builds use stock holiman/uint256 v1.3.2 MulMod. The unreleased v2.12.0-beta and develop route full-width moduli through a one-entry reciprocal memo (core/vm/mulmod_memo.go:55-66), and its result equals MulMod for every input because MulModWithReciprocal and MulMod run the same reduce4 call on the same reciprocal (uint256.go:688-748). No second client would catch a future fault in that path. Today every vector, script and mined swap matches.

What is at risk: None today. If a future bor release computed a wrong MULMOD or shift result, the chain would accept it, and swaps would pay amounts that differ from the source. Who gains depends on the fault.

What it takes: A fault would have to ship in a bor release that block producers run. No ordinary account can cause one.

What can be done: Before each bor release that touches core/vm (the MULMOD memo of v2.12.0 first), rerun battery.py, targeted.py, run_edges.py and the memo sequences of cycles/026/established-l1.work/memoseq.py against a node on that release.

Code: `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:24`, `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:48`, `src/libraries/SqrtPriceMath.sol:252`.

Sources:

- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/0xPolygon/erigon/v3.7.2/polygon/chain/chainspecs/bor-mainnet.json (the last Erigon chain spec ends at valenciaBlock 89531000)
- https://forum.polygon.technology/t/bor-v2-9-0-and-erigon-v3-7-2-releases/21987 (Erigon v3.7.2 shipped with Valencia in lockstep with bor v2.9.0)
- https://docs.tatum.io/changelog/polygon-erigon-eol-migration-to-bor (Polygon Labs ended 0xpolygon/erigon support on 1 August 2026 (secondary))
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/0xPolygon/bor/develop/params/config.go (Austin 91949700, Uburu 96787900 and Salvador 98688600 on mainnet)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/0xPolygon/bor/develop/core/vm/mulmod_memo.go (memo logic at lines 55-66)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/holiman/uint256/v1.3.2/uint256.go (MulModWithReciprocal and MulMod differ only in where the reciprocal comes from)

## F-160. Celo public endpoints answer most reads from superseded celo-reth releases, one of them older than a consensus fix

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: sequencer, validator or bridge committee. Networks: Celo.

What differs on the network: On Celo the stakely.io endpoint answers from four reth builds and onfinality from one, and the most frequent of them is the oldest public release. In 500 batches stakely.io answered 286 from 7680d6d (celo/reth-v1.0.0 or v1.0.1), 173 from 74cc8bb (v1.0.9 or v1.0.10) and 41 from 06140ff (v1.0.6 to v1.0.8). 3cbf835 (v1.0.3) answered none of the version-only batches but 307 of the eth_call batches of the pinned vector run, so it is still in the rotation. 7680d6d predates the v1.0.4 change that makes a zero-address feeCurrency an unregistered currency and adds the CIP-64 rule balanceOf(caller) >= gasLimit * maxFeePerGas.

How it meets the code: A wallet, router or quoter that reads the PoolManager through these endpoints gets an eth_call answer from whichever build serves the batch. Each batch is served whole by one backend (0 mixed batches of 500), and the backend changes in runs of 11 to 66 batches. All four builds lock revm-interpreter 35.0.1 and ruint 1.18.0 or 1.20.0. Pinned this way every build returned the specified word on every vector it was pinned to: 7680d6d on 214,437 of 215,637 vectors in cycle 28, 3cbf835 on 4,200 in cycle 28, 74cc8bb on all sets, and 06140ff this cycle, which returned the specified word on 20400 of 215637 vectors pinned before the run was stopped (34 of 360 batches, 0 mismatches; while it ran, the eth_call batches were answered 2654 times by 74cc8bb, 1830 by 7680d6d, 307 by 3cbf835 and 34 by 06140ff). The three getFeeGrowthInside borrow scripts (src/libraries/Pool.sol:498-509) gave the reference record on 7680d6d and 74cc8bb, and the 23 multi-transaction scenarios gave the reference outputs on 7680d6d (22 of 23 scenarios identical (90608 bytes compared), not identical: E1; E1 differs only in where revert data is reported) and on 74cc8bb. The builds differ in CIP-64 admission and pricing of simulated fees, which no PoolManager line reads. Blocks come from the Celo sequencer. 7680d6d and 3cbf835 are the looser builds: they accept every transaction the v1.0.4 rule accepts, and more, so a follower on one of them never rejects a block from a sequencer on v1.0.4 or later. A split needs a sequencer on an old build that includes a transaction the v1.0.4 rule rejects; the newer followers would then reject that block, as op-geth did at block 75,046,581.

What is at risk: No PoolManager funds move and no withdrawal is blocked. A user relying on 7680d6d may see a CIP-64 simulation priced differently from the chain, or a zero-address feeCurrency transaction accepted by that node and then rejected.

What it takes: The user reads or simulates through stakely.io or onfinality. A divergent block needs a sequencer that runs one of the old builds.

What can be done: Integrators should check web3_clientVersion and read the PoolManager through an endpoint that reports reth/v2.2.0-74cc8bb or later; Celo can ask providers to retire builds older than v1.0.4.

This entry adds to F-116.

Second reviewer's corrections: The source line says v1.0.0 locks celo-org/reth 7680d6d. The lockfile pins rev 7680d6d of paradigmxyz/reth. The divergence sentence needs a fix on direction. 7680d6d and 3cbf835 are the looser builds: they accept every transaction the v1.0.4 rule accepts, and more. A follower on an old build therefore never rejects a block from a v1.0.4+ sequencer. A split happens only if an old-build sequencer includes a transaction that v1.0.4 rejects, and then the newer followers reject it, as op-geth did at block 75046581. The onfinality claim (4 of 4 calls answered by 7680d6d) could not be re-tested because of rate limiting. The actor (operator) and the severity (info) are right.

Code: `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:24`, `src/libraries/TickMath.sol:61-63`, `src/libraries/Pool.sol:498-509`.

Sources:

- https://celo-json-rpc.stakely.io (500 batches of three web3_clientVersion calls on 2026-11-27: 286 batches 7680d6d, 173 74cc8bb, 41 06140ff, 0 mixed (out/rotation-stakely.json, out/rotation-stakely-2.json))
- https://celo.api.onfinality.io/public (web3_clientVersion answered reth/v2.2.0-7680d6d to 4 of 4 calls on 2026-11-27)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/celo-org/celo-kona/refs/tags/celo/reth-v1.0.0/Cargo.lock (v1.0.0 pins revision 7680d6d of paradigmxyz/reth and ruint 1.18.0 (read in cycle 28))
- https://github.com/celo-org/celo-kona/tags (newest tag is still celo/reth-v1.0.10 of 20 November 2026; there is no v1.0.2 tag)
- https://github.com/celo-org/celo-kona/tags?after=celo%2Freth-v1.0.5 (v1.0.4 makes a zero-address feeCurrency an unregistered currency (read in cycle 28))

## F-163. The main branch of holiman/uint256 replaces division and remainder on the path every go-ethereum family network takes, and no client pins it yet

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: sequencer, validator or bridge committee. Networks: Ethereum, BNB Smart Chain, Polygon, Avalanche, Arbitrum One, Robinhood Chain, Elysium, Celo.

What differs on the network: holiman/uint256 master de2a038 (2026-11-22, proxy version v1.3.3-0.20261122165954-de2a0381370e) adds a power-of-two shortcut to Div, new register-only routines udiv and umod with one Knuth step per divisor size (rem5by4, rem4by3, quoRem4by3, rem3by2), and rewrites SDiv, SMod, Slt, Sgt, Eq, WriteToSlice and PaddedBytes. Mod is now umod, and MulMod and AddMod reach it whenever the product or sum fits in 256 bits. go-ethereum master bbafd66, bor develop, bsc develop, celo-org/op-geth master, OffchainLabs/go-ethereum master and nitro master all still require v1.3.2, and avalanchego 1.15.3 and libevm main require v1.2.4.

How it meets the code: 1. The PoolManager reaches these routines on every swap and liquidity change: DIV and MOD in UnsafeMath.divRoundingUp and TickMath, SDIV and SMOD in TickBitmap.compress and flipTick, MULMOD in FullMath.mulDiv and mulDivRoundingUp, SLT and SGT in the tick checks. 2. When a client bumps its uint256 requirement, the go-ethereum handlers call the new code with the same operand aliasing as today (y.Div(x, y), z.MulMod(x, y, z), instructions.go of go-ethereum master). 3. A fault in the new Knuth steps would change a quotient or a remainder for divisors of a particular limb count, which would change prices, amounts and tick indexes in mined blocks on that network. 4. This cycle built v1.3.2 and master with the local go1.24.7 and ran 1,845,864 vectors through both, with the aliasing of the go-ethereum handlers, against Python integers: no mismatch in either. The set includes every edge pair, every power of two and its neighbours as divisor, divisors of one to four limbs with the top limb normalised and not, quotients at a limb boundary, FullMath shapes and the 21,537 MULMOD and ADDMOD records of cycle 25. Three planted faults that change results were caught (6, 669 and 26,444 mismatches); a fourth planted change turned out to be equivalent and changes nothing.

What is at risk: None today. No network runs the new code. If a release adopts it with a fault the tests above missed, every pool on that network would compute different amounts from other clients and from the reference, which is a consensus split before it is a loss to any user.

What it takes: A client release that requires a uint256 version later than v1.3.2. None does as of 2026-11-27.

What can be done: Rerun scripts/u256vectors.py of this work directory against the uint256 version of each geth-family release before deployment, and the battery and targeted sets against an endpoint that runs it.

Code: `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:24`, `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:48`, `src/libraries/UnsafeMath.sol:12-16`, `src/libraries/TickBitmap.sol:21-29`, `src/libraries/TickBitmap.sol:59-74`.

Sources:

- https://proxy.golang.org/github.com/holiman/uint256/@v/master.info (master is de2a0381370e of 2026-11-22T16:59:54Z)
- https://proxy.golang.org/github.com/holiman/uint256/@v/v1.3.3-0.20261122165954-de2a0381370e.zip (uint256.go Div lines 641-683 (power-of-two shortcut, udiv), Mod 687-704 (umod), MulMod 818-846, AddMod 245-311; div.go udiv, umod, rem5by4, rem4by3, quoRem4by3, rem3by2)
- https://proxy.golang.org/github.com/holiman/uint256/@v/v1.3.2.zip (the v1.3.2 routines that every client pins)
- https://proxy.golang.org/github.com/ethereum/go-ethereum/@v/v1.17.11-0.20261125134423-bbafd66b66f5.zip (go-ethereum master bbafd66 go.mod line 43 requires github.com/holiman/uint256 v1.3.2; core/vm/instructions.go handlers call Div, SDiv, Mod, SMod, AddMod, MulMod with the result aliased to an operand)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/0xPolygon/bor/develop/go.mod (bor develop requires uint256 v1.3.2)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/bnb-chain/bsc/develop/go.mod (bsc develop requires uint256 v1.3.2)

- local run: research/network-risk/cycles/029/theme-geth-family-caches-and-slot-walk.work/results/u256-planted.json (planted faults in rem5by4 and rem3by2 caught (6 and 669 mismatches); the y[2] mask change is an equivalent mutant (0))

## F-164. Nethermind's next release pins an integer library eleven days old that rewrites division and remainder and adds Barrett reduction to MULMOD, and no public endpoint lets anyone test a Nethermind node

Info severity, confirmed. Effect: degrade. Who can trigger it: sequencer, validator or bridge committee. Networks: Ethereum.

What differs on the network: Ethereum validators run five execution clients with their own 256 bit arithmetic. Nethermind, which reports 25 to 43 percent of validators, publishes 2.3.0-rc on 2026-11-23 with Nethermind.Numerics.Int256 1.15.0 (published 2026-11-16), against 1.11.0 in the current release 2.2.2. The new version changes 24 division and remainder routines, adds three, and adds Barrett reduction for six named moduli and a pseudo-Mersenne fold for moduli 2^256 - c with c below 2^64 - 1. One added test in Remainder512By256Bits and Remainder512By192Bits drops the leading quotient digit when the dividend's top limb is below the divisor's top limb, which is the kind of shortcut that broke ruint 1.21.0. No public Ethereum endpoint names Nethermind, so none of the 200,127 vectors or the script sets has run on it.

How it meets the code: Every swap and liquidity change reaches MULMOD through FullMath. mulDiv computes mulmod(a, b, 2^256 - 1) first (src/libraries/FullMath.sol:24); Nethermind sends that modulus to a fold of the high half into the low half, present unchanged since 1.11.0, before any Barrett or pseudo-Mersenne test. The remainder mulmod(a, b, denominator) at FullMath.sol:48 and :112 and at SqrtPriceMath.sol:252 can reach the new reductions only when a times b is at least 2^384. Read against the callers, that never happens: Position.sol:94-96 multiplies a 256 bit fee growth by a 128 bit liquidity, SqrtPriceMath.sol:47, :71 and :208-209 multiply a liquidity shifted by 96 (below 2^224) by a price below 2^160, SqrtPriceMath.sol:97 and :105 multiply an amount by 2^96, and SwapMath.sol:65, :74 and :104 multiply by at most 10^6. The 4 limb remainder routine is reached at SqrtPriceMath.sol:44-47, where the denominator numerator1 + amount x price can be any value up to 2^256 - 1. Its new leading digit test is correct: with N the dividend, d the divisor and m = uLen - 4, the leading digit is zero exactly when floor(N / b^m) < d, and a top limb below d3 implies that, before or after normalisation. A Python transcription of that routine in both the hardware division arm and the reciprocal arm gave the exact remainder for 489,674 cases, 73,660 of them shaped as SqrtPriceMath.sol:44-47, and a planted <= in place of < gave 586 mismatches. The reciprocal, 64 bit, pseudo-Mersenne, all ones fold, signed remainder, limb negation and power of two exponent routines gave no mismatch on 2,914,162 cases, and six planted faults were caught. A wrong result on a Nethermind node would make it compute a different post-state for a block that contains the swap. The client cannot steal: geth and the other clients hold the majority, so the Nethermind nodes would reject canonical blocks or propose blocks the rest reject. If more than one third of validators ran the faulty build, finality would stall until it was fixed.

What is at risk: No loss of pool funds. A fault would cost Nethermind validators missed duties and could delay finality; balances on the canonical chain follow the clients that compute the specified result.

What it takes: A fault in a Nethermind Int256 routine that this transcription does not cover (Remainder512By192Bits, MulModBy128Bits, the 128 and 192 bit dividers, the .NET JIT output) and validators that upgrade to 2.3.0 with that fault. An ordinary account cannot choose which client builds or validates its block.

What can be done: Nothing for the deployer or LPs on Ethereum. Node operators who use Nethermind can stay on 2.2.2 until 2.3.0 final has run the consensus test suites; the review can rerun the transcriptions on the version 2.3.0 final pins.

This entry adds to F-163.

Code: `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:21-26`, `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:46-49`, `src/libraries/FullMath.sol:109-116`, `src/libraries/SqrtPriceMath.sol:38-47`, `src/libraries/SqrtPriceMath.sol:250-253`, `src/libraries/Position.sol:92-97`, `src/libraries/SwapMath.sol:65`.

Sources:

- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NethermindEth/nethermind/2.3.0-rc/Directory.Packages.props (2.3.0-rc pins Nethermind.Numerics.Int256 1.15.0)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NethermindEth/nethermind/2.2.2/Directory.Packages.props (2.2.2 pins Int256 1.11.0 (line 69))
- https://github.com/NethermindEth/nethermind/releases/tag/2.3.0-rc (2.3.0-rc is a pre-release published 2026-11-23 with 1,282 merged pull requests)
- https://api.nuget.org/v3/registration5-gz-semver2/nethermind.numerics.int256/index.json (Int256 1.15.0 published 2026-11-16, 1.11.0 on 2026-10-06)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NethermindEth/int256/v1.15.0/src/Nethermind.Int256/UInt256.divmod.cs (MultiplyMod, MultiplyModWideCore with the 2^256 - 1 fold, Remainder512By256Bits with the new leading digit test, the reciprocal and hardware dividers)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NethermindEth/int256/v1.15.0/src/Nethermind.Int256/UInt256.barrett.cs (Barrett and pseudo-Mersenne reductions)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NethermindEth/int256/v1.15.0/src/Nethermind.Int256/UInt256.std.cs (Remainder512ByFullModulus takes the new reductions only when the high half of the product is at least 2^128 (lines 392-412))
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NethermindEth/int256/v1.11.0/src/Nethermind.Int256/UInt256.DivideMod.cs (the 2^256 - 1 fold already present in 1.11.0)
- https://clientdiversity.org/ (execution client shares: Nethermind 25.46 percent in one survey and 43 percent in another)
- file:research/network-risk/cycles/031/ethereum-reference.work/out/neth-int256.json (2,914,162 cases of the transcribed routines, 0 mismatches; planted faults in out/neth-int256-plant1.json to plant6.json)
- file:research/network-risk/cycles/031/ethereum-reference.work/out/neth-rem512.json (489,674 cases of Remainder512By256Bits, 0 mismatches; out/neth-rem512-plant1.json shows 586 mismatches with the planted <=)

## F-023. Celo governance can move native CELO out of the PoolManager through the CELO token proxy and the transfer precompile

High severity, confirmed. Effect: both. Who can trigger it: owner, fee controller or chain governance. Networks: Celo.

What differs on the network: On Ethereum no contract can debit ETH from another account. On Celo a precompile at 0xfd transfers native CELO from any address named in its input to any other address. The precompile accepts calls only from the CELO token address 0x471EcE3750Da237f93B8E339c536989b8978a438. That address is an upgradeable proxy owned by the Celo Governance contract.

How it meets the code: The PoolManager keeps native CELO as its own account balance. Pools that use currency address(0) and pools that use the CELO token address both draw on that balance (src/types/Currency.sol:90-96, and the on-chain balances are equal). The precompile reads the debited account from its first argument and checks only the caller. The current token implementation passes msg.sender or an allowance checked from address. A new implementation installed through _setImplementation could pass the PoolManager address as from and any recipient as to. The PoolManager has no code that runs on such a debit and no way to veto it. After the debit, take for either CELO currency pays out until the balance is exhausted (src/PoolManager.sol:291-297), so the last LPs to withdraw bear the loss. A new implementation that makes transfer revert would freeze every pool that uses the CELO token address, because take calls transfer on it (src/types/Currency.sol:60-86). Pools that use address(0) would still pay out by value transfer in that case. Governance returns stage durations of 1 day, 7 days and 3 days, which this review reads as approval, referendum and execution, so a standard proposal is public for days before it can execute. The hotfix path needs the approver multisig and the Security Council and has a 1 day execution window with no referendum.

What is at risk: All native CELO in the PoolManager, 6,167.7 CELO at the time of the query, across both currency faces. Every CELO holder on the chain carries the same exposure. Other tokens in the PoolManager are not reachable by this path.

What it takes: A Celo governance proposal or a hotfix approved by the approver multisig and the Security Council must replace the CELO token implementation. No unprivileged path was found: an eth_call to the precompile from an address other than the token fails, and _setImplementation reverts for a sender other than Governance.

What can be done: Watch the value of _getImplementation() on the CELO token proxy and the Governance contract for queued proposals and hotfixes that target 0x471E...a438. The referendum stage gives LPs days to withdraw before a standard proposal executes. A hotfix gives no such window. LPs who want protection from a token contract that reverts can use pools with currency address(0), which pay out without calling the token.

Second reviewer's corrections: 1. The impact is wider than stated for pools that list the CELO token as a currency. A replaced implementation could report a larger balanceOf between sync and settle. _settle credits the difference to the caller (src/PoolManager.sol:349-364), and the caller can swap that credit for the paired currency in every pool that lists the CELO token and take it out. The statement that other tokens are not reachable holds for the precompile debit alone. Pools on address(0) are exposed to the precompile debit alone. 2. The hotfix path needs 3 of 14 approver signers and the Security Council. The Security Council address is the alias of the Ethereum Safe that also owns Celo's ProxyAdmin on both layers, so one signer set sits in the predeploy upgrade path and in the CELO token hotfix path. 3. If transfer reverts, an LP in a CELO token pool can still turn a positive delta into ERC-6909 claims with mint (src/PoolManager.sol:322-329), which makes no token call. The claim stays inside the PoolManager, so this is bookkeeping and gives no exit. 4. The balance was 6,166.94 CELO at my read against 6,167.7 in the finding. 5. Severity stays high because the actor is chain governance. The exposure today is small and is shared by every CELO holder.

Code: `src/types/Currency.sol:90-96`, `src/types/Currency.sol:40-88`, `src/PoolManager.sol:291-297`, `src/PoolManager.sol:279-288`.

Sources:

- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/celo-org/celo-kona/HEAD/crates/celo-revm/src/precompiles/transfer.rs (The precompile checks that the caller is the CELO token address and takes from, to and value from its input.)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/celo-org/op-geth/HEAD/core/vm/celo_contracts.go (The former op-geth client implements the same rule: IsCallerCeloToken, then a transfer between the from and to addresses decoded from the input.)
- https://docs.celo.org/operate/specification/token-duality.md (The precompile manipulates balances in the state database directly and can only be called by the CELO token.)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/celo-org/celo-monorepo/master/packages/protocol/contracts/common/GoldToken.sol (balanceOf returns the native balance, and transfer and transferFrom call the precompile with an abi encoded from address. The master branch reports version 1.3.0.0 while the deployed implementation reports 1.2.0.0, so the deployed source may differ in detail.)
- https://forno.celo.org eth_call _getOwner() and _getImplementation() on 0x471EcE3750Da237f93B8E339c536989b8978a438; eth_call getAddressForString('Governance') on 0x000000000000000000000000000000000000ce10 (The proxy owner is 0xD533Ca259b330c7A88f74E000a3FaEa2d63B7972, which the registry lists as Governance. The implementation is 0xFeA1B35f1D5f2A58532a70e7A32e6F2D3Bc4F7B1.)
- https://forno.celo.org eth_call _setImplementation(address) on the CELO proxy from Governance and from 0xA1; eth_call to 0xfd from the token address and from 0x01 (The implementation setter succeeds for Governance and reverts with 'sender was not owner' otherwise. The precompile succeeds for the token address as caller and fails for another caller.)
- https://forno.celo.org eth_getBalance and CELO.balanceOf for 0x288dc841A52FCA2707c6947B3A777c5E56cd87BC (Both return 6167711480450733670741 wei.)
- https://forno.celo.org eth_call stageDurations(), hotfixExecutionTimeWindow(), approver(), securityCouncil() on 0xD533Ca259b330c7A88f74E000a3FaEa2d63B7972 (Stage durations are 86,400, 604,800 and 259,200 seconds and the hotfix execution window is 86,400 seconds. The meaning of the three stages was not checked against the Governance source.)

## F-138. Arbitrum governance can post a contract transaction whose sender is the PoolManager, and Nitro runs it without a nonce or code check, so every pooled balance can be moved without unlock

High severity, confirmed. Effect: both. Who can trigger it: owner, fee controller or chain governance. Networks: Arbitrum One, Robinhood Chain, Elysium.

What differs on the network: On Ethereum only a key or the contract's own code can move what a contract holds. On Nitro chains the sender of an L1-to-L2 message is a field that the Inbox contract on the parent chain fills in, and the Inbox is upgradeable by the chain owner. On 2026-10-30 the Arbitrum Security Council (9 of 12, no delay on the emergency path) used a temporary Inbox implementation with sendUnsignedTransactionOverride to send two type 0x65 transactions from 0x3e2bf1ac6bf0934bbba6456c341c2ecbced632d5, which moved 74,559,805.2 ARB and 8,425.99 ETH. Nitro builds both unsigned user transactions and contract transactions with From set to the poster field of the message (arbos/parse_l2.go lines 245-269). The contract transaction type skips nonce checks and transaction checks (core/types/arb_types.go lines 66 and 77 of fork 0e44603), and the check that rejects a sender with code sits inside the transaction checks (core/state_transition.go preCheck), so a contract transaction may name an address that holds code.

How it meets the code: An override Inbox would enqueue a message of kind ContractTx with the PoolManager address as sender, the address of a pooled token as destination and the calldata of transfer(recipient, balance), or a native value transfer with empty calldata. Nitro turns it into an ArbitrumContractTx from the PoolManager. Neither the nonce check nor the EOA check runs, so the call executes as an outer call whose msg.sender is the PoolManager. The token sees an ordinary transfer by its holder. Nothing in the PoolManager runs: no unlock, no delta accounting, no NonzeroDeltaCount check, so pools, positions and ERC-6909 claims still record balances that are gone. An eth_call from the PoolManager address to USDC with transfer(0x...0da0, whole balance) at block 527,417,705 returns true, which shows that the token itself places no hurdle on such a call. The type 0x65 path the Council used would fail for the PoolManager, because unsigned user transactions keep the EOA check (arb_types.go line 76); the contract transaction path does not. On Robinhood Chain the chain owners and the rollup admin on Ethereum hold the same path, and on Elysium the single chain owner and the Inbox admin on HyperEVM do.

What is at risk: Every balance the PoolManager holds on the three chains, which on Arbitrum One at block 527,417,705 includes 4,405.32 ETH and 9,252,963.77 USDC. Liquidity providers and claim holders of every pool would be left with records that cannot be paid out.

What it takes: The Arbitrum Security Council emergency signers (9 of 12) or the DAO timelock on Arbitrum One, the chain owners or rollup admin on Robinhood Chain, or Elysium's owner. An ordinary account cannot reach it: the stock Inbox aliases the sender of every unsigned and contract message by adding 0x1111000000000000000000000000000000001111, and producing the PoolManager address would need control of a fixed parent chain address with no known key.

What can be done: None in core. Integrators and LPs should treat these chains' upgrade path as able to spend any contract's balance, as the Binance freeze showed for an EOA. Off-chain monitors can watch for type 0x65 and 0x66 transactions whose sender is the PoolManager.

Second reviewer's corrections: Balances moved since the finding's block: at 527,442,078 the PoolManager holds 4,395.53 ETH and 9,029,518.54 USDC. The eth_call test skips the EOA check by design and so shows only that USDC places no hurdle; the contract transaction path itself rests on the client source. The same parties can already change ArbOS state by an upgrade, so this adds a cheaper and already exercised route rather than a new class of power. For Robinhood Chain and Elysium the deployed Inbox implementations on their parent chains were not read in this check.

Code: `src/PoolManager.sol:104-114`, `src/PoolManager.sol:291-297`, `src/types/Currency.sol:40-90`.

Sources:

- https://forum.arbitrum.foundation/t/security-council-emergency-action-30-10-2026/31625 (The Council upgraded the Inbox to add sendUnsignedTransactionOverride, impersonated the sender of two cross-chain transactions and restored the Inbox in one atomic action.)
- https://defiprime.com/arbitrum-council-freezes-binance-exploit-funds (The Arbitrum One leg was transaction 0x75e81a... of type 0x65 with nonce 2 and zero signature fields.)
- https://arb1.arbitrum.io/rpc eth_getBlockByNumber 519514683 and 519514685, run 2026-11-25 (Type 0x65 transactions 0x75e81a23...eeedb6 (ARB transfer to 0x...0da0) and 0x410af166...5f70 (ETH to 0x...0da0) from 0x3e2bf1ac...32d5 with r = 0.)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OffchainLabs/nitro/master/arbos/parse_l2.go (UnsignedUserTx and ContractTx are built with From set to the message poster.)
- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OffchainLabs/go-ethereum/0e44603/core/types/arb_types.go (ArbitrumContractTx returns true from skipNonceChecks and skipTransactionChecks; ArbitrumUnsignedTx returns false for both.)
- research/network-risk/cycles/016/arbitrum-family.work/dl/st.go:748-758 (Copy of the fork's core/state_transition.go read in cycle 16: the ErrSenderNoEOA check runs only when SkipTransactionChecks is false.)
- https://arb1.arbitrum.io/rpc eth_call from 0x360e68fa...fb32 to USDC 0xaf88...5831 transfer(0x...0da0, 9252963766169) at block 527417705 (Returns true; eth_getBalance of the PoolManager is 4,405.32 ETH.)

## F-140. OP Stack portal upgraders can emit a deposit whose sender is the PoolManager, and the clients run it without nonce or code checks, as OP Mainnet governance did for another address on 2026-10-31

High severity, confirmed. Effect: both. Who can trigger it: owner, fee controller or chain governance. Networks: Optimism, Base, Unichain, Zora, Worldchain, Ink, Soneium, Celo, X Layer, MegaETH.

What differs on the network: On Ethereum only a key or the contract's own code can move what a contract holds. On OP Stack chains the from field of a deposit transaction (type 0x7e) is whatever the OptimismPortal on Ethereum writes into the TransactionDeposited event, and the portal is an upgradeable proxy. op-geth skips the nonce check and the sender-has-code check for deposits (core/state_transition.go preCheck, the IsDepositTx branch returns before both checks), and op-revm handles a deposit in validate_against_state_and_deduct_caller without calling validate_account_nonce_and_code (handler.rs lines 130 to 161). A deposit from an address that holds code therefore runs as an outer call from that address. On Ethereum block 26,309,074 (2026-10-31 00:10:59 UTC) the OP Mainnet ProxyAdmin owner Safe 0x5a0aae59d09fccbddb6c6cceb07b7279367c3d2a, in transaction 0xd80476b3ec56283723804cf7bc2d96f800c81d3ebd0fea843555396ffe94f28f, upgraded the OP Mainnet portal to the unverified implementation 0x6d971b07cea5febaea8aefb827032f6a0538c9bb, emitted TransactionDeposited with from 0x5227f603c1ee34ce3c06efe6477003bdfccb2f8c, to the OP token predeploy 0x4200000000000000000000000000000000000042, and calldata transfer(0x6b1bae59d09fccbddb6c6cceb07b7279367c4e3b, 44,231,858.7 OP), and restored implementation 0x1005217ad392dc64cef501fa1777a27d42166748 in the same transaction. The recipient is the L1-to-L2 alias of the Safe. The temporary implementation hardcodes SENDER, RECIPIENT and AMOUNT and checks CALLER. Cycle 20 addition, MegaETH: the same holds there. The OptimismPortal2 proxy 0x7f82f57F0Dd546519324392e408b01fcC7D709e8 on Ethereum is upgradeable through ProxyAdmin 0x15fcb0120d414f246ead019ca4bdf97447cd8d90, whose owner is the Safe 0x92e0e0b15e3e99b32c9ed9ad284f939553c7b7d6 with a threshold of 6 of 10. mega-evm v1.7.4 hands deposits to op-revm at revision f67d87cd, which returns from the deposit branch before the nonce and code validation. Cycle 20 addition, warning time: the portal ProxyAdmin owners of Celo and X Layer are plain Safes with no module and no guard. Celo's is a 2 of 2 Safe whose owners are two 6 of 8 Safes, and X Layer's is a 2 of 3 Safe of EOAs, so an upgrade executes as soon as it is signed.

How it meets the code: An upgrade of the same kind with the PoolManager address as SENDER, a pooled token as target and transfer(recipient, balance) as calldata, or a native value transfer with a mint, would make the L2 execute a call whose msg.sender is the PoolManager. The token sees a transfer by its holder. Nothing in the PoolManager runs: unlock (src/PoolManager.sol:103-113), the delta accounting of take (src/PoolManager.sol:291-297) and the NonzeroDeltaCount check are bypassed, so pools, positions and ERC-6909 claims keep records of balances that are gone. On every one of the nine chains the stock portal sets from to msg.sender only when msg.sender equals tx.origin or carries a 23-byte EIP-7702 delegation, and to msg.sender plus 0x1111000000000000000000000000000000001111 otherwise, so an ordinary account cannot produce the PoolManager address: the PoolManager address on Ethereum has no code and nonce 0 on all nine chains, it is a CREATE-derived or predeploy address with no known key, and its alias preimage has no code and nonce 0. Cycle 20 addition: on MegaETH the verified implementation 0x55400445e384393f9c1be23e7e734e8d44ed9fd9 (version 3.15.2) sets from to msg.sender, or to its alias unless msg.sender equals tx.origin or carries an EIP-7702 delegation, so an ordinary account cannot produce the PoolManager 0xacb7e78fa05d562e0a5d3089ec896d57d057d38e: that address and its alias preimage 0x9ba6e78fa05d562e0a5d3089ec896d57d057c27d have no code and nonce 0 on Ethereum. The PoolManager's nonce on MegaETH is 1, so no deposit has been sent in its name. On Celo the verified source of implementation 0x2c431080 (version 5.1.1) emits the event only in _depositTransaction and in setGasPayingToken, which only SystemConfig may call. The Safe transaction service listed no pending upgrade for the Celo or X Layer owner Safe on 2026-11-25.

What is at risk: Every balance the PoolManager holds on the nine chains. Liquidity providers and claim holders would hold records that cannot be paid out. The OP Mainnet action shows the path is operational and was used within a day of the Arbitrum Security Council action recorded in F-138. Cycle 20 addition: the same on MegaETH, which brings the count to ten networks. On Celo and X Layer nothing on chain gives liquidity providers time to withdraw after an upgrade is signed.

What it takes: The owner of the portal's ProxyAdmin on Ethereum: 0x5a0aae59d09fccbddb6c6cceb07b7279367c3d2a for OP Mainnet, Unichain, Ink and Soneium; 0x7bb41c3008b3f03fe483b28b8db90e19cf07595c for Base; 0x4a4962275df8c60a80d3a25faec5aa7de116a746 for Zora; 0xa4fb12d15eb85dc9284a7df0adbc8b696edbbf1d for World Chain; 0x4092a77baf58fef0309452ceacb09221e556e112 for Celo; 0xc290be56089bcc83c6993583ce2cf51a7951d45a for X Layer (eth_call owner() on each ProxyAdmin read from the EIP-1967 admin slot). No ordinary account can reach it. On MegaETH: six of the ten owners of Safe 0x92e0e0b15e3e99b32c9ed9ad284f939553c7b7d6.

What can be done: None in core. Integrators and LPs should treat the portal upgrade path of these chains as able to spend any L2 contract's balance. Monitors can watch Upgraded events on the nine portal proxies and TransactionDeposited events whose from equals the PoolManager address.

Code: `src/PoolManager.sol:103-113`, `src/PoolManager.sol:291-297`, `src/types/Currency.sol:40-80`.
